Current implementation
----------------------
include/ckks/narrow_bootstrapping.hpp (also included by tfhe++.hpp):

* CKKSNarrowBootstrapSchedule tracks selector, plaintext-product, balanced
  product-tree, and output levels at compile time. The public beta is a
  transparent product-tree leaf, avoiding a separate final plaintext level.
  The paper's h=31 therefore uses exactly five tree levels.
* Sections 3 and 4: head tables, packed guard/tail tables, complex-packed
  signed selectors, conjugation after accumulation, tail alignment, two
  balanced product trees, and beta weighting.
* Every product-tree multiplication uses CKKSDDRelinKeyChain and the
  existing Double Decomposition CKKS tensor/rescale/relinearization path.
  CKKSNarrowBootstrapSchedule's optional bool selects DD
  relinearization before rescale (default false). True uses input-level
  DD keys and CKKSMultRelinThenRescale. Ciphertext/tree levels and the
  five-level h=31 depth are unchanged; evaluation setup keys must be
  generated for the selected order. Derived FFT chains support both.
* The optional auxiliary-scale argument lifts public ciphertext
  coefficients for factor/output conjugation and whole tail rotation chains,
  then divides once. S2C and dense-to-sparse encapsulation likewise run at
  the raised scale, with one final division under the sparse secret. Data
  levels stay fixed; evaluation keys use the actual larger moduli, which
  must be included in security tuning. The default zero preserves the
  previous pipeline. This uses the existing DD keys and exact arithmetic.
* Section 5: prepared selector limbs in one or two auxiliary NTT primes. Public
  transformed base tables are encoded/transformed once per factor; every
  candidate uses the fixed permutation for X -> X^(5^-y). Accumulations
  are reconstructed exactly before torus rescaling. A checked integer
  convolution bound selects one prime whenever sufficient, saving setup
  memory and arithmetic. Otherwise use two-prime CRT.
  These auxiliary primes support degree 65536. They are scratch moduli,
  not cryptographic RNS ciphertext moduli. The implementation checks the
  integer convolution bound. The coefficient reference path remains
  available when the prepared cache is empty.
* Section 6: four coefficient-packed indicator ciphertexts, reusable
  substitution keys, binary-tree coefficient extraction, division by N,
  and evaluator-only reconstruction of the narrow head/tail selectors.
  Reconstruction consumes nbit + plain_log_delta bits above selector_log_q.
* Section 7: CKKSNarrowEvalSeries directly generates each positively
  weighted frequency using the same selectors, then uses conjugation and
  addition for negative frequencies. No ciphertext powers or exponential
  approximations are used. The caller supplies offline Fourier coefficients.
* CKKSNarrowBlindRotateBatch and CKKSNarrowEvalSeriesBatch share each W_x
  across multiple input/frequency columns. They use a tiled sparse W*U
  multiplication with one selector load reused across batch columns.
* Optional derived FFT caches cover factor/output conjugation, required
  tail rotation bits, and every DD product-tree level. Prepare them from
  the existing public raw keys after generation or loading:
    CKKSNarrowPrepareAutomorphisms<Narrow>(key.narrow);
    CKKSNarrowPrepareTailRotations<Narrow>(key.narrow);
    CKKSNarrowPrepareRelinearization<Narrow>(key.narrow);
  Packed extraction additionally supports
    CKKSNarrowPreparePackedSubstitutions<Narrow>(packed, levels=2);
  This prepares only the deepest requested substitution depths from public
  raw keys. Zero disables it; invalid depth throws without replacing caches.
  Packed-key serialization omits the caches and loading/rekeying clears them.
  They require the multi-limb digit FFT backend. Raw keys remain the
  serialized form; caches are cleared on loading and rekeying. Rebuild
  after changing raw keys. These caches are optional because of their
  quadratic decomposition-row storage cost.

include/ckks/narrow_refresh.hpp:
* One-level BSGS S2C in natural slot order, followed by key switching from
  the input dense key to the structured sparse key at the lowest modulus.
* Compressed provisioning can generate reusable refresh setup without
  encrypting expanded selectors:
    CKKSNarrowRefreshSetupKeyGen<Refresh>(key, input, sparse, evaluation);
    CKKSNarrowPackedKeyGen<Narrow>(packed, sparse, evaluation);
    CKKSNarrowReconstructSelectors<Narrow>(key.narrow, packed);
  The standalone narrow setup API is CKKSNarrowSetupKeyGen<Narrow>.
  Setup removes old selectors and derived caches; reconstruction is required
  before evaluation. Packed indicators and setup can be stored independently.
* Full complex slot-to-slot CKKSNarrowRefresh: SHIP's gamma/(4*pi*i) beta
  weighting and conjugation recover the sine approximation of each real
  and imaginary message. Both halves share the narrow keys and DD tree.
* X^-N/2 exposes the imaginary coefficient half. X^N/2 combines the real
  results into complex CKKS slots without an extra rescale or key switch.
* CKKSNarrowFunctionalRefresh performs componentwise functional refresh,
  or real-only functional refresh, using shared input/frequency batches.
* Refresh keys and offline approximants support cereal serialization.

include/ckks/narrow_approximation.hpp:
* Offline discrete trigonometric Remez exchange, positive-frequency
  coefficient construction, and independent shifted-grid validation.
* A supplied target Lipschitz bound additionally gives a between-grid
  uniform error bound. Encrypted evaluation error is separate.

Representation details
----------------------
TFHEpp decrypts b-a*s, while the paper uses b+a*s: negate the public input
mask for the paper's table construction. TFHEpp's slot-rotation API rotates
left, while the paper rotates right: use negative rotation arguments.
Neither source nor evaluation secret is accessible to the evaluator.
The source key must have exactly one +/-1 coefficient in every public block.
Use ceil(N/h)-wide blocks and a shorter final block for the paper's h=31.
Absent candidate positions in the last block have zero selectors, including
when reconstructed from the four coefficient-packed ciphertexts.
The evaluation key may be independent of the source key.

Serialization omits the derived NTT cache. CKKSNarrowPrepareKey rebuilds
it after loading; evaluation without it uses the coefficient reference path.
Call PrepareKey again after modifying selectors.

Verification
------------
test/ckks/ckks_narrow_bootstrapping.cpp:
* Exhaustive block/offset head and tail table checks against negacyclic
  convolution, including final partial tail packs (N=64, h=16, B=5).
* Exact NTT permutation checks against coefficient automorphisms.
* Noisy encrypted roots for N=16,32,64 at frequencies 1 and 3, with an
  independent evaluation secret and complex beta weights.
* A real Fourier series with complex positive-frequency coefficients.
* Every decompressed head/tail selector compared to independent encryption;
  encrypted blind rotation using the reconstructed key.
* Rejection of malformed structured source keys.
* Uneven source blocks, odd product trees, both one- and two-prime NTT paths.
* Mixed-input/frequency batches with a partial final batch tile.
* Full complex refresh, S2C natural-order coefficient validation, a
  subsequent DD multiplication, and a second bootstrap cycle.
* Exact Remez recovery of a known Fourier polynomial; tanh approximation
  on [-4,4], independent-grid validation, and encrypted functional refresh.
* The h=31 table geometry is explicitly checked at N=512.
* Portable-binary refresh-key round trips clear preexisting derived caches.
  Evaluation works immediately after loading through the coefficient path;
  rebuilding the NTT cache reproduces the original ciphertext exactly.
* Portable-binary packed-key round trips feed the encrypted selector
  reconstruction tests. Offline approximation round trips preserve all
  coefficients and validation metadata and feed functional refresh.
Checks throw on failure even when NDEBUG is defined.
These serialization regressions passed in /tmp/ckks-narrow-serialization-test.log.
The nonlinear FBS regressions now encrypt inputs spanning the full [-4,4]
approximation interval, including both endpoints. Degree-six sampled Remez
errors are 0.00424664 (Tanh), 1.74035e-5 (Sigmoid), and 0.000197149 (GeLU).
The encrypted complex outputs pass a separate 6e-4 evaluation-error gate
against the approximants and the combined validated approximation/evaluation
bounds against the targets (/tmp/ckks-narrow-nonlinear-test.log). These are
small-ring functionality checks, not Table-7 security/performance results.

Build (from TFHEpp, with normal compiler/Boost/CMake prerequisites):
  cmake -S . -B build -DENABLE_TEST=ON -DUSE_BLOCK_BINARY=OFF -DUSE_AVX512=OFF
  cmake --build build --target ckks_narrow_bootstrapping ckks_bootstrap_primitives -j2
  OMP_NUM_THREADS=2 ./build/test/ckks/ckks_narrow_bootstrapping
  OMP_NUM_THREADS=2 ./build/test/ckks/ckks_bootstrap_primitives

This machine lacks installed CMake and Boost headers. Ubuntu packages were
downloaded and extracted to /tmp/ckks-build-deps/root; CMake was run with
LD_LIBRARY_PATH pointing to its usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu and the compiler's
standard include directories extended with its usr/include. Native GCC13
was sufficient. The available codex.sif lacks build dependencies and its
fakeroot mode has no subuid mapping, so it was not used for compilation.

Realistic validation in progress
-------------------------------
test/ckks/ckks_narrow_benchmark.cpp is an opt-in h=31 Gaussian-noise benchmark.
--small uses N=512; --ll13 uses N=8192. It performs a full complex refresh
and a subsequent DD multiplication, reports setup/online time, NTT-cache
bytes, maximum/RMS error, and precision, and writes JSON when given a path.
No arguments skip the expensive benchmark so the ordinary test runner
does not silently launch a security-sized workload.

The LL13-inspired fixed-scale DD port uses q0=2^25, S2C plain scale 2^20,
input scale 2^21 (gamma=16), selector Q=2^184, tree/output scale 2^20,
five 20-bit tree rescalings, and output Q=2^64. Bg/Bbar are 4-bit, with
absolute Gaussian standard deviation at least 3.2 at every level. The
four-ciphertext packed setup key has Q=2^(204+nbit), at most 2^217 for
N=8192. This is an explicit port schedule, not the paper's SEAL RNS chain.
Security is not marked validated without an assessment of the actual
exposed key moduli and structured sparse source distribution.

This initial 20-bit profile FAILS accuracy validation. Keep its reports as
negative evidence: narrow-dd-small-gaussian-fft.json records maximum error
1.83759 at sigma=3.2; narrow-dd-small-zero-noise-fft.json records maximum
error 0.198388 even without encryption noise. The respective CPU refresh
times are 3.3888 and 3.39669 seconds at N=512. These are functional profiling
measurements, not secure parameter settings or paper performance claims.
Stage diagnostics show Gaussian S2C max error 0.0427054 and first-factor max
error 0.0935315. DD tensor rounding before relinearization also matters.

Parameter tuning belongs in the sibling Parameter-Selection repository,
as requested by the user. There is no parameter/security tuning Python
implementation in TFHEpp. Parameter-Selection/python/narrowCKKSnoise.py
reuses the existing GL variance utilities and screens/searches the current
raw-gadget schedule. It now includes an average-case BSGS S2C/encapsulation
model; FFT error and security remain excluded. Its bounded LL13 search found
no candidate even at the paper's 4-bit target;
this is not an impossibility result. The separate security script in
python/estimates/narrow_ckks_security.py uses that repository's pinned
lattice-estimator (3e48ef421ec256afddb3e7d2249a77eab6e9ba12). Completed dense
selector/packed-key generic estimates are 153.99/128.30 classical bits.
The sparse-source proxy results are incomplete; security is not validated.

DD FFT processors now also support ring degrees 512 and 8192 with separate
thread-local scratch. Exact signed integer convolution checks at both
degrees pass, together with narrow functional tests and existing CKKS
bootstrap primitives. Saturating the FFT fusion-count shifts fixes the
compile-time overflow exposed by small decomposition bases. The FFT path
reduces the small benchmark's refresh from roughly 103 to 3.4 seconds.

LL14 candidate and memory-bounded validation
------------------------------------------
Parameter-Selection selected N=16384, h=31, q0=48, input scale=41,
S2C scale=37, selector Q=379, root plaintext scale=34, and tree scale=49.
Five tree rescalings leave output Q=100. The coefficient-packed setup key
has Q=427, inside the paper's 437-bit LL14 key-modulus budget. Storage is
seven 64-bit limbs, with Bg=Bbar=4 bits and absolute Gaussian sigma=3.2.
The supplied message domain is [-0.125,0.125] for each complex component;
gamma=128. This is a DD port profile, not SEAL's exact RNS chain.

The companion model adds tensor-rescale rounding, raw gadget key error,
selector/plaintext encoding, BSGS switch counts, sparse encapsulation,
and the complex sine bound. Eight RMS plus the sine bound is only an
engineering screening criterion, not a tail/failure proof. The selected
candidate is recorded in Parameter-Selection/python/estimates/
narrow_ckks_ll14_candidate.json. Its completed generic dense selector and
packed-key estimates are 150.01 and 131.60 classical bits; the sparse-source
proxy remains incomplete. No 128-bit structured-secret proof is claimed.

CKKSNarrowCacheMode::Streamed prepares one public selector block at a time,
reusing its NTT matrix over all input/frequency columns in the tile. Its
results match the fully prepared mode bit for bit. The chosen full cache
would use 239,628,976,128 bytes; a streamed block uses 6,656,360,448 bytes.
Separate limb outputs allow parallel NTT matrix evaluation without torus
coefficient races. Selector key generation and cache preparation also run
in parallel. Large refresh/product ciphertext temporaries have been moved
to the heap; the remaining large-ring runtime failure is under diagnosis.
Packed selector reconstruction now visits the coefficient extraction tree
depth first, retaining only O(log N) packed ciphertexts in addition to the
final selectors. It consumes each leaf immediately instead of retaining
four N-element leaf arrays and a breadth-first frontier.
Encrypted reconstruction, fully prepared/streamed equality, coefficient
reference batches, and the full functional-refresh regression all passed
after this change (/tmp/ckks-narrow-dfs-test.log).

Raw and seeded CKKS key switching now evaluates only secondary digits whose
shifts are below the active modulus. The original centered decomposition
offset is retained, including carries at unaligned levels. Products match
independent integer negacyclic convolution at active levels 17, 85, 379, and
448 bits, with nonzero unused high bits and signed 4-bit multipliers. For
the LL14 S2C level, this reduces the secondary products per row/component
from 112 to 22. The narrow encrypted regression and generic CKKS bootstrap
primitives pass (/tmp/ckks-narrow-active-test.log and
/tmp/ckks-primitives-active-test.log). This changes arithmetic work, not
the noise/parameter profile; tuning remains in Parameter-Selection.

--small-high validates this same schedule at N=512. The Gaussian run in
narrow-dd-small-high-parallel.json passed: maximum refresh error 9.32408e-7
(20.03 bits), RMS error 4.05017e-7, subsequent DD product error 3.09304e-7.
It is a correctness test at a smaller ring, not a security-sized result.
After active-level secondary products, narrow-dd-small-high-active.json also
passed: maximum refresh error 1.02757e-6 (19.89 bits), RMS 4.1151e-7, and
subsequent DD product error 3.40792e-7. It ran concurrently with the large
GDB diagnostic, so its 46.6945-second refresh time is not comparable to the
earlier isolated timing and does not establish a speed improvement.

Full-domain functional validation and curvature bound
----------------------------------------------------
The benchmark now accepts --small-high-{tanh,sigmoid,gelu}-full and
--ll14-{tanh,sigmoid,gelu}-full, with degree-six approximants on [-4,4].
These use input scale 44 (gamma=16), retaining the DD selector/tree levels.
Full-domain endpoints are explicitly encrypted. Offline approximation time
is reported separately from encrypted evaluation. Reports now include DD
square error against the approximant as well as the target function; gates
account for amplification according to |a^2-b^2| <= 2|b||a-b|+|a-b|^2.
These degree-six approximants are low-precision target approximations even
though encrypted evaluation uses the high-precision DD schedule. They do
not reproduce Table-7 high-precision target accuracy.

CKKSBuildNarrowTrigonometricApproximation accepts an optional target second-
derivative bound as its final argument. The residual's linear-interpolation
error is bounded by curvature*spacing^2/8; the smaller of this and the
Lipschitz bound is returned with a floating-point allowance. The supplied
bounds remain caller assumptions. Coefficients are unchanged. Regression
session 14211 passed with exit 0, including a separate 32769-point tanh
validation grid (/tmp/ckks-narrow-curvature-test.log).

Full-domain Gaussian N=512 runs, launched before the curvature change,
are still live at the last observation: tanh session 2995, sigmoid 70100,
GeLU 48090. Logs are /tmp/ckks-narrow-{tanh,sigmoid,gelu}-full.log;
intended reports docs/narrow-dd-small-high-{tanh,sigmoid,gelu}-full.json.
These runs use the Lipschitz-only approximation bound. The latest build
(session 13584, /tmp/ckks-narrow-curvature-build.log) adds the curvature
bound to subsequent benchmarks. Poll each existing handle before retrying.
Large-ring baseline tanh session 26167 remains live, in S2C after key setup
completed in 250.874 seconds. It predates accumulated conjugation and all
full-domain/curvature benchmark changes.

Full-frequency streamed FBS reuse

complex_input in both overloads. The unary CKKSNarrowEvalSeries also exposes
it, so real-only refresh propagates the same control. Default remains eight;
zero tiles are rejected. A wider tile reuses each streamed W_x across more
frequency/input columns, retaining more product-tree factors in memory.
The benchmark selects 2*degree columns for complex FBS, allowing the whole
frequency batch to share selector preparation, and reports series_tile_size.
Regression checks compare default/wide complex ciphertexts, prepared versus
streamed wide evaluation, and real-only single-column versus wide tiles
bit for bit. Real-only outputs are also checked against the approximant.

Slot-to-slot batch APIs
-----------------------
CKKSNarrowRefreshBatch and CKKSNarrowFunctionalRefreshBatch accept a span
of independent slot ciphertexts and reuse the public S2C plan and narrow
keys. All real/imaginary input halves and positive-frequency columns share
selector preparation within the supplied tile size. Functional batches
support coefficient vectors or offline approximants, and real-only inputs.
Standard batch refresh is the one-frequency gamma/(4*pi*i) sine series.
Example, with Refresh the schedule and inputs a vector of Refresh::Input:
  std::vector<Refresh::Output> outputs;
  CKKSNarrowRefreshBatch<Refresh>(outputs, inputs, key, 8);
  CKKSNarrowFunctionalRefreshBatch<Refresh>(outputs, inputs, key,
                                         approximation, true, 24);
The slot-batch build (session 71852) and regression (93098) passed with exit
0. Logs: /tmp/ckks-narrow-slot-batch-build.log and
/tmp/ckks-narrow-slot-batch-test.log. Tests prove ciphertext equality against
independent unary evaluations for standard, complex functional, and real-only
functional batches. They exercise partial tiles, streamed selectors, distinct
inputs, and clearing outputs on an empty standard batch.
Remaining functional-level audit: the LL14 standard-refresh schedule leaves
Qout=2^100 at scale 2^49; after a fixed-scale DD square this is Q=2^51 at
scale 2^49. Centered coefficient capacity is therefore only 2. Gaussian
slot samples do not establish correctness for large constant output slots:
for example GeLU(4)+i*GeLU(4) squares to nearly 32i, whose constant imaginary
coefficient exceeds that capacity. Verify constant-slot functional outputs
and select a matching retained-level/output-amplitude budget in Parameter-
Selection before treating the full-domain FBS profile as generally usable
with a remaining level. The completed square reports prove their sampled
inputs only, not a uniform retained-level guarantee.

Retained functional-level range correction
------------------------------------------
Parameter-Selection now provides minimum_square_output_bits and
retained_square_range, plus --range-only / --retained-component-bound /
--retained-guard-bits CLI options. For arbitrary complex slots with both
components bounded by R, the square has magnitude <=2*R^2 and every inverse-
encoded coefficient is bounded by that magnitude. Fixed-scale DD leaves
centered coefficient capacity 2^(Qout-2*scale-1). Strict containment plus
three guard bits selects Qout=108 for R=4.01 and scale 49, selector Q=387,
and packed Q=435 at N=16384. Range reports are in the companion repository:
python/estimates/narrow_ckks_ll14_fbs_range.json (old profile fails) and
narrow_ckks_ll14_fbs_range_candidate.json (revised profile passes).
Five companion model tests passed, including the strict centered-boundary
case and malformed bounds. Ordinary searches can now enforce this range.
This is an amplitude budget and does not supply a nonlinear noise proof.

The benchmark uses selector Q=387 / output Q=108 for new functional runs;
standard-refresh parameters are unchanged. Existing reports and live runs
retain their original profile. The new profile has a distinct JSON label,
ll14-fbs-range-dd-streamed, and input_pattern distinguishes constant endpoints.
The --retained-range diagnostic completed with exit 0 after rebuild session
46069: docs/narrow-dd-retained-range.json shows encoding constant 32i at the
old post-square Q=2^51, scale 2^49 gives error 32; revised post-square Q=2^59
at the same scale gives error 1.95943e-15. This is direct range evidence,
not an encrypted full-bootstrap result.

--small-high-gelu-constant is running in session 3946 with the revised
schedule and every complex input slot equal to 4+4i. It performs degree-12
FBS and a DD square, exposing the worst-case constant imaginary output.
Log: /tmp/ckks-narrow-gelu-constant.log; intended report:
 docs/narrow-dd-small-high-gelu-constant.json. The old --small-high-tanh-precise
session 57436 and current-code LL14 tanh diagnostic 78638 remain live.

report Parameter-Selection/python/estimates/narrow_ckks_ll14_fbs_security.json.

Windowed periodic Remez option (validation in progress)
------------------------------------------------------
A C8 smoothstep taper can constrain the Fourier extension outside the
message interval while leaving the target exactly unchanged inside it.
Standalone offline fits to a tanh extension, protected domain [-4,4],

Contiguous-column NTT matrix scratch layout
------------------------------------------
The public transformed table matrix and NTT accumulators in
packedFactorsBatch now store batch columns contiguously at each NTT point.
The W*U inner loop still loads one selector and reuses it across columns,
but its table reads and accumulator writes no longer stride by the ring
degree. NTT input/output columns are gathered/scattered at transform
boundaries. Selector spectra, serialization, digit arithmetic, modular
multiplications, CRT reconstruction, and rescaling are unchanged.

/tmp/ckks-narrow-matrix-layout-test.log.

column-major matrix implementation using the same encrypted keys/tables.

under GDB) retain their earlier executable and are not affected by this
layout change. Poll those handles before starting any retry.

Next performance audit: raw auto key switching decomposes each row key into
secondary digits and transforms it during every call. Factor conjugation
and tail alignment call that path repeatedly across blocks/frequencies.
The existing prepared DD relin FFT switch accumulates frequency products
across primary rows before inverse transforms; analogous derived auto-key
FFT caches could avoid repeated setup while preserving the original full-
torus offsets at unaligned moduli. Assess this with actual timing and exact
integer/ciphertext comparisons before changing the noise/parameter model.

Prepared automorphism implementation (2026-10-06)
------------------------------------------------
include/ckks/prepared_auto.hpp derives an FFT cache from existing public
CKKSAutoKey rows. It preserves both original full-torus centered offsets,
active row counts, and physical shifts. Evaluation transforms the primary
input digits once and accumulates all primary-row products before each
secondary inverse FFT. A compile-time mantissa headroom check limits the
supported parameters. This does not regenerate keys or change moduli/noise.

Call CKKSNarrowPrepareAutomorphisms<Schedule>(key) after key generation to
enable cached factor and output conjugations. This is optional and requires
the multi-limb digit FFT backend. Tail rotations still use raw keys. The
derived caches are omitted from serialization and cleared on loading and
rekeying; rebuild them after loading or modifying raw keys. Cache memory
is quadratic in the active Bbar row count, so this is not enabled by default.

85,379,448, Gaussian row keys, unused high key/input bits, and serialization
cache invalidation. Log: /tmp/ckks-narrow-prepared-auto-test.log.

seconds respectively; cache bytes were 204800,3964928,73932800,102760448.
These are diagnostic observations, not controlled speedup estimates.

Further performance work can use the existing CKKSDDRelinKeyFFT evaluator
for the product trees: the raw DD path currently calls PolyMulDigit per
primary/secondary row and rounds each product separately. The FFT evaluator
already accumulates primary products before inverse transforms, but its
factory regenerates rows from a secret. A public raw-row-to-FFT conversion
and optional narrow DD-chain cache would preserve existing keys and enable
exact differential testing. This has not been implemented or validated.

Prepared Double Decomposition product trees (2026-10-06)
-------------------------------------------------------
The public raw-row conversion is now an overload of makeCKKSDDRelinKeyFFT
in ckks.hpp. The existing secret-key factory delegates to it. Conversion
transforms the already decomposed raw rows, without encryption, secret-key
access, RNG draws, or changes to the digit conventions. The converter and
chain factory check mantissa headroom including primary-row accumulation.

include/ckks/prepared_dd.hpp supplies an optional derived FFT cache for every
level of an existing CKKSDDRelinKeyChain. Call
CKKSNarrowPrepareRelinearization<Schedule>(key) after generating/loading the
raw narrow key. The product trees use the cache when present, preserving
the DD tensor/rescale/relinearization algorithm. The cache is excluded from
serialization and cleared when loading or regenerating a key. Existing raw
evaluation remains the default. Callers must rebuild after editing raw rows.

Prepared tail rotation chains (2026-10-06)
----------------------------------------
include/ckks/prepared_auto.hpp now supports selected binary rotation caches
and delegates rotation normalization/composition to the existing binary-chain
helper. Missing required cache bits are rejected. The cache derives only from
existing public auto keys, preserving their full-torus offsets and raw switch
arithmetic. CKKSNarrowPrepareTailRotations<Schedule>(key) computes the union
of binary bits used by all tail pack extraction shifts and prepares those
keys only. For N=512,h=31 it needs bits 5,6,7; for N=16384,h=31 it needs
bits 5,6,7,10,11,12. This avoids preparing unused rotation keys, but memory
per cached bit remains quadratic in the active Bbar row count.

Tail alignment uses this optional cache when present. Serialization omits
it; loading and rekeying clear it. Rebuild after modifying any raw Galois key.
Regression session 5988 exited 0: all eight N=512,h=31 tail shifts at Q=85
matched the raw chain exactly, including zero shifts, wrapped/negative
arguments, and in-place output. Only the three required bits were prepared
(11894784 bytes). Missing-bit rejection and serialization invalidation also
passed, as did the DD tree and full existing regression. Evidence:
/tmp/ckks-narrow-prepared-rotation-test.log. git diff --check passed.

Coverage audit after the rotation-cache change
---------------------------------------------
The passing regression is implementation evidence for Sections 3--6 and
the direct-frequency variant of Section 7, including integer NTT equivalence,
DD trees, and selector decompression. It is not a full realistic-profile
validation. In particular, main() invokes testEncrypted (which includes the
packed-key generation/reconstruction path) only at N=16,32,64. The h=31,
N=512 test invokes testTables only. The opt-in benchmark generates expanded
selectors directly, so its packed_log_q report and the generic packed-key
security screen do not prove an h=31 compressed-key refresh. A realistic
compressed-key reconstruction/refresh remains to be run and inspected.

Full N=16384 standard refresh and a retained DD square have passed in
docs/narrow-dd-ll14-active.json. Precise sigmoid/GeLU and the constant GeLU
retained-range case have passed at N=512, not N=16384. Full-domain precise
tanh is still pending in the windowed runs; the failed direct degree-20
report must not be counted as a pass. Large-ring nonlinear results and
security-sized batch validation remain missing. Existing small-ring batch
checks establish equality to independent unary evaluations only.

The implementation is a CPU multi-limb DD port. It does not supply the
paper's GPU kernels or demonstrate its latency results. The optional
order-two frequency-basis variant in Section 7 is also not implemented;
the direct positive-frequency path is implemented and exercised. Current
dense generic estimates for selector/packed moduli are screens only; the
structured-source public-block distribution, ring attacks, and circular/KDM
assumptions are outside their scope. The sparse proxy screens remain
incomplete. Keep the full goal active while these validation gaps remain.

Fixed-multiplier matrix kernel
-----------------------------
Multi-column W*U now prepares the existing modular_ntt::ShoupMultiplier once
per shared selector value and reuses it for the batch columns. Both NTT
spectra are canonical residues and the supported primes are below 2^63,
matching that helper's contract. The one-column path keeps the original
wide modular multiplication, enabling exact encrypted batch/unary checks.
This avoids one wide division per column after a single quotient setup;
the selector cache representation and integer CRT/rescale are unchanged.

Public mask reuse during extraction
-----------------------------------
CKKSNarrowReconstructSelectors now lazily encodes each public head or tail
mask once and reuses it at all coefficient leaves. It retains the same
encoded polynomial, plaintext multiplication/rescale, and selector addition;
there is no change to extraction keys, ciphertext levels, or serialization.
The masks are local to one reconstruction call, and unused tail masks are
not allocated. This removes repeated slot encodings rather than changing
the four-ciphertext packing algorithm. Build session 13252 exited 0 and
built both targets. Regression session 15987 is live with log
/tmp/ckks-narrow-mask-reuse-test.log. Small realistic-profile validation is
also running: /tmp/ckks-narrow-small-high-packed-mask-reuse.log, eventual
docs/narrow-dd-small-high-packed-mask-reuse.json.
Large-ring session 97182 remains live and predates mask reuse; its result
will validate the previous executable. No speedup claim is made before
controlled measurement.

helpers. The compressed benchmark now calls setup, generates/round-trips
the four indicators plus public substitution keys, and reconstructs; it
does not generate expanded selectors first. Previous packed benchmark
reports retain their documented redundant-generation limitation.

not passing evidence for LL13. The scale/modulus budget and accumulated
switching/tensor noise must be investigated before selecting a new profile.

Isolated noisy switch and balanced-tie diagnostic
------------------------------------------------
--ll13-switch-noise isolates one conjugation switch at N=8192,Q=45,
delta=21,sigma=3.2 using 32 independent encrypted masks and one reused
public key. It compares switched slots to the conjugate of the decrypted
input, excluding fresh input/encoding error. Raw/prepared exact comparison
covers the first input. Primary digit means and per-slot mean errors are
reported separately from total RMS. No production decomposition changed.

Across these three keys, balanced ties reduce persistent peak error
from0.45..0.80 to0.10..0.11. Overall RMS remains about0.17 and max switch

coefficient-dependent tie choices distribute the bias rather than making
it zero per coefficient over repeated ciphertexts. Production changes
require broader decomposition/key-switch/product-tree validation.

The next arithmetic investigation should examine rescale/relinearization
ordering: current CKKSMult rounds the tensor components before switching
s^2, amplifying that rounding by the dense squared secret. This is a
source-level observation and a candidate experiment, not a measured fix.
The existing CKKSTensorProductRescale permits LogScale=0, so an alternative
can be tested with DD switching at the input modulus followed by ordinary
rescale, preserving the requested DD arithmetic and the accuracy gate.

DD multiplication ordering: diagnostic and explicit API
------------------------------------------------------
--ll13-multiplication-order compares identical inputs at N=8192,Q=64,
delta=20, output Q=44, using eight random complex inputs per zero-noise
and sigma3.2 key mode. Expected values multiply decrypted inputs, excluding
fresh input/encoding error. The alternative tensors modulo input Q, DD
relinearizes at that input Q, then rescales its two components. Default
CKKSMult remains unchanged. Build52382 and diagnostic96561 exited0.
docs/narrow-dd-ll13-multiplication-order.json reports:
zero-noise early/late RMS=0.1944653 / 0.00185288, max=2.3944095 / 0.0119031;
sigma3.2 early/late RMS=0.3894200 / 0.00183922, max=2.5348295 / 0.0114705.

CKKSMultRelinThenRescale is now an explicit public API in ckks.hpp. It
requires a relinearization key at min(input moduli), sufficient headroom
for the doubled/product scale before rescale, and returns the existing
CKKSMultResult type for mixed levels/scales. It preserves the same DD
key formats and leaves the old multiplication API unchanged. Narrow
product-tree key schedules have not yet been integrated with this API.

Opt-in late-rescale narrow tree integration
-----------------------------------------
CKKSNarrowBootstrapSchedule<..., HammingWeight, true> selects input-level
DD relinearization followed by two-component rescale at every tree product.
Relin-chain starting metadata shifts up by one scale, so key levels are
factorQ-level*delta instead of factorQ-(level+1)*delta. The highest new DD
key level is factorQ, already used by factor Galois setup; selector/packed
moduli and all ciphertext levels remain unchanged. The default false
schedule retains the original order and key types. Both raw and prepared
DD tree dispatches follow the schedule. Setup must be generated for the
chosen order; an old output-level DD chain cannot serve the new schedule.

exactness, an uneven h=7 encrypted tree, and packed full complex refresh/
serialization/functional batches using late rescale. The realistic h=31
benchmarks enable all three FFT caches and stream NTT selectors. Their
post-refresh square deliberately uses default CKKSMult, verifying that
new-tree outputs remain usable by existing consumers. JSON field
relinearize_before_rescale refers to the narrow product-tree schedule.

LL13's remaining modeled error includes automorphism/S2C switching rather
than solely product-tree rounding. A next investigation may check existing
hybrid sparse Galois infrastructure or a DD-compatible raised-modulus
key switch with auxiliary division. Such keys must stay within the true
largest public-key modulus/security budget, and the model must account

validated by the late-rescale results; do not assume SEAL's suppressed
key noise for the current ordinary DD automorphism keys.

Scaled DD automorphism primitive and prospective LL13 screen
----------------------------------------------------------
Existing CKKSHybridSparseGaloisKey combines direct/binary rotations; it is
not a modulus/noise-suppressed switch. CKKSEvalAutoWithAuxiliaryScale now
provides a separate primitive in prepared_auto.hpp: lift public ciphertext
coefficients by2^A into levelQ+A and scaleDelta+A, use the existing raw or
prepared DD auto key atQ+A, then divide both components by2^A. Input phase/
encryption error is preserved; evaluation-key error is divided by2^A and
final component rounding remains. A>0 and Q+A within the torus storage
are enforced. Security tuning must count the actual larger key modulus.
The primitive does not change serialized key formats or existing APIs.

2026-10-06 16:19 UTC: integrated auxiliary-scale DD refresh passes LL13
------------------------------------------------------------------------
CKKSNarrowBootstrapSchedule now accepts a final optional AuxiliaryLogScale
(default0), in addition to its optional RelinBeforeRescale flag. Factor and
output automorphism keys/caches use their data modulus plus this scale.
Conjugation and each whole binary tail rotation chain lift canonical public
ciphertext components, evaluate with the existing raw/prepared DD key at
that larger modulus, and divide once. Data/tree/output levels are unchanged.
CKKSNarrowRefreshSchedule lifts the complete one-level BSGS S2C and dense-to-
sparse encapsulation pipeline, then divides once under the sparse secret.
Fresh input noise is preserved; this does not divide away encryption error.
Setup, serialization, cache rebuild, and compressed reconstruction retain
public evaluator-only behavior. No hybrid-RNS key or new serialized format
is introduced. Count the actual larger evaluation-key levels in security.

Noise-model audit found that factor variance omitted tail-alignment binary
rotation chains. It now includes the longest public shift's popcount (6
for LL13/LL14); a raised chain sums its key-switch variances and divides
only once, rather than adding one rounding per binary step. A zero shift
performs no key switch or division. The tail factor screen is conservatively
applied at every tree leaf. These independent average-case key variances
are not a shared-key covariance or whole-run tail certificate.

plus-sine screen1.8600834e-5, above2^-16. The model test now records this
failure and checks the new schedule at the same gate, instead of loosening

2026-10-06: public disk-backed selector source and complete memory audit
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Previous turn was progress (encrypted HT retained-level diagnostics).
CKKSNarrowSelectorSource is an optional public group-loader interface in
narrow_bootstrapping.hpp. CKKSNarrowSelectorFile in narrow_selector_file.hpp
stores complete head/tail groups in portable fixed-size cereal records.
Each file checks N, data level, scale, root plaintext scale, storage width,
group width, head/tail kind, and group index before loading fixed-size
ciphertexts. Truncated/trailing data is rejected without trusting an
arbitrary serialized vector length. Directories must be new; cleanup
removes only known cache files and preserves unrelated files. The new
header is included by tfhe++.hpp.

CKKSNarrowStoreSelectors exports existing public selectors exactly, then
releases raw vector storage and NTT caches. CKKSNarrowEncryptSelectorsToFile
uses setup-side source/evaluation secrets to encrypt one group at a time;
reusable evaluation keys must already exist. Evaluation requires neither
secret and loads one group, optionally transforms it to the same NTT cache,
and performs the existing reference/matrix factor computation. External
sources are omitted from key archives and reset on load/rekey/compressed
reconstruction. A loaded file-backed key therefore needs its public source
reattached or regenerated; its archive alone contains evaluation setup, not
the external selector payload. Existing in-memory archive fields/format are
unchanged. File-backed compressed reconstruction is explicitly unsupported
in the benchmark; ordinary compressed reconstruction remains available.

2026-10-06: exact compact DD storage and compact S2C implementation

New include/ckks/compact_dd.hpp provides an explicit opt-in DD format.
Balanced secondary digits use int8/int16/int32 for Bbar<=8/16/32. Packing
checks both the balanced range and the complete native torus coefficient;
upper-limb garbage and non-digits are rejected. Direct key generation
allocates only one primary row's temporary decomposition, not the full raw
quadratic matrix. Raw switching expands one polynomial at a time; FFT
preparation uses the same digits and existing derived key type. Compact DD
chains support both multiplication orders and archives with checked layout
metadata. The default schedule and legacy raw archive remain unchanged.

Rotation cache accounting for next work: the number of public binary shifts
changes with ring dimension, so multiplying an N512 total rotation-cache
size by Nfull/Nsmall is not a valid full-size estimate. For LL14 h31,
N512 tail_width16 uses three bits; N16384 tail_width528 uses eight. A
bounded cache should prepare one binary key, apply it across all matching
columns/tail leaves in ascending bit order, free it, and round only once
after the complete auxiliary lift/rotation chain. HT15 also needs separate
peak accounting for the group NTT cache versus derived auto/rotation keys;
compact plaintext/key payload sizes alone do not establish that it fits.

packedFactorsBatch now accepts an optional positive selector chunk size.
It transforms each bounded chunk once, reuses its entries across all input/
frequency columns, and retains canonical modular sums across chunks. Inverse
NTT, CRT, native torus recomposition and rounding occur only after the whole
group has accumulated. The original full-cache/reference paths retain their
arithmetic; the same modular product loop serves full and chunked paths.
Chunk caches are destroyed before native digit recomposition/conjugation.
CKKSNarrowUseSelectorChunks is a public opt-in, clears full selector caches
and selects streamed mode; its runtime chunk policy is omitted from legacy
archives and reset on load/rekey. Both in-memory and external selector
sources support it. The benchmark first-factor diagnostic also uses chunks
rather than accidentally allocating the complete group NTT cache.

2026-10-06: staged FFT cache implementation

Prior chunked turn was concrete progress (new bounded NTT evaluator,

CKKSNarrowUseStagedFFT now selects streamed selector chunks (default40),
bounded tail rotations, and clears permanent factor/output/rotation/DD FFT
caches. Per tile, BlindRotateBatch owns one local factor FFT cache shared by
all groups/columns. It releases this before preparing a local DD-chain FFT
for all product trees in that tile. Those local keys die before output
conjugation; the conjugation helper prepares one temporary output FFT key.
Evaluation leaves the public key const and unchanged. Direct factor diagnostics
also use temporary FFT keys. Runtime stage policy is omitted from legacy
archives and reset on load/rekey. Explicit full-cache preparation selects
that full-cache policy again. Default behavior and arithmetic stay unchanged.

Paper Section7 was re-read from /tmp/2026-2119.txt: its optional order-two
basis computes unweighted z^a, reconstructs missing positive frequencies
as z^a*z^b, applies c_k after reconstruction, and needs one extra product
level. Any future implementation must preserve this order and account for
coefficient precision, its changed logical scale, final output conjugation
key level, and retained-square range. The present direct-frequency API
already applies c_k in beta and must remain available with its existing gates.
A frequency-basis path is not yet implemented; no such performance or
full-ring validation claim is made. Source Section8.1 describes CPU AVX2/
OpenMP on SEAL; current measurements are CPU TFHEpp observations and have
not been controlled for contention or matched to the paper's latency.

Optional Section7 public exponent planner added to sibling Parameter-Selection
(no ciphertext arithmetic changed). Exact exhaustive positive order-two search
supports degrees1..40, permits repeated summands, and excludes signed sums.
The search starts at the singleton+unordered-pair cardinality lower bound,
explores increasing members no larger than the first uncovered frequency,
and prunes only by the maximum number of remaining singleton/pair positions.
Degree12 smallest basis is {1,3,5,6} (4 roots,8 extra products,21 nodes).
Degree40 smallest basis is {1,3,4,9,11,16,17,19,20} (9 roots,31 products,
18752 nodes across the rejected size8 and successful size9 searches).
All frequencies have explicit singleton or pair reconstructions; coefficients
are applied AFTER reconstruction in the algebra checks. Four tests pass: all
supported degrees cover every frequency; small-degree minima independently
checked by unpruned combinations; arbitrary complex weights and conjugation
match direct series; invalid inputs rejected. Existing11 noise tests pass.
Saved plans: Parameter-Selection/python/estimates/narrow_ckks_frequency_basis.json.
Encrypted basis evaluation, coefficient-scale budget, final conjugation key,
extra DD level and security validation remain outstanding. These plans are
not encrypted performance or accuracy evidence.

Optional Section7 ciphertext frequency basis IMPLEMENTED (small-ring scope).
include/ckks/narrow_frequency_basis.hpp validates positive order-two plans,
produces unweighted z^a with the existing public blind-rotation evaluator,
reconstructs missing z^(a+b) with one extra compact DD product at inputQ,
then rounds coefficients to signed fixed-point integers and multiplies by
r+i*s exactly using X^(N/2)=i at the canonical powers-of-five slot roots.
No extra coefficient rescale: logicalDelta becomes rootDelta+CoefficientBits,
outputQ becomes rootQ-rootDelta. A separate outputQ conjugation key is used
after accumulating all weighted positive terms, then public c0 is added.
Types expose this new modulus/scale and reject insufficient product headroom.
Additional public product/conjugation keys have explicit archive metadata.
Derived product FFT lifetime ends before output conjugation FFT preparation.
Public evaluation accepts no secret keys, and existing direct-frequency API
and gates remain available. tfhe++.hpp exports the new header.

include/ckks/narrow_refresh.hpp now wraps this reconstruction with the same
S2C/encapsulation pipeline: FunctionalRefreshWithBasis and Batch, including
real-only and componentwise complex inputs and an approximation unary overload.
BasisRefreshSchedule exposes the changed output type explicitly.
